{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LcUVfTYg6QHWE2AkTXqGLk","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# MedOne Healthcare Partners — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-14\n     4→Developer website: https://www.medonehp.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→MedOne Healthcare Partners (formerly Central Ohio Hospitalists) is a physician-owned hospital medicine practice founded in 2000 in Columbus, Ohio. It is **not a traditional health IT vendor** — it is a clinical practice that built its own EHR software to support its operations. The organization has grown to be the largest hospital medicine practice in central Ohio, with over 100 physicians, 75+ advanced practice clinicians, and 40+ administrative staff. They partner with 4 acute hospital systems, 4 long-term acute care hospital partners, and approximately 150 skilled nursing, assisted living, and independent living facilities across Ohio.\n     9→\n    10→MedOne's founder, Dr. Joseph Mack, is both a physician and programmer. In 2012, he also founded a separate entity, **MedOne Systems, LLC** (based in Marietta, OH), which develops the **BOLT** clinical software platform — an EHR overlay/enhancement tool designed to augment existing enterprise EHRs. The certified product **OneConnect** appears to be a related but distinct tool from BOLT, specifically focused on post-acute care documentation and workflow. Both products share the same origin and development philosophy: clinician-built software designed for hospitalist and post-acute care workflows.\n    11→\n    12→MedOne Healthcare Partners is a very small vendor from a health IT certification perspective. OneConnect appears to be primarily used internally by MedOne's own clinicians to document care across the post-acute facilities they serve — it is not marketed as a commercial EHR product for external customers. The company has approximately $3M in annual revenue (MedOne Systems) and no presence on standard EHR review platforms (G2, KLAS, Capterra).\n    13→\n    14→## Product: OneConnect\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 11426\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→OneConnect is a **custom clinical documentation tool** designed for post-acute care settings. According to MedOne's own description, it is \"a custom documentation tool that enables providers to streamline workflows, communicate effectively and improve patient care and experience.\" Critically, it is **designed to integrate seamlessly with a post-acute facility's existing EMR** — meaning OneConnect is not a standalone full-featured EHR but rather a documentation overlay used by MedOne's physicians as they round across skilled nursing facilities, assisted living facilities, rehab hospitals, and long-term acute care hospitals.\n    21→\n    22→The certified module (Version 0, certified 2023-12-27) covers a focused set of ONC criteria — 24 criteria in total, including CPOE for medications (a)(1), demographics (a)(5), implantable device list (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1), EHI export (b)(10), clinical quality measures (c)(1), standardized FHIR APIs (g)(7)/(g)(9)/(g)(10), and Direct messaging (h)(1). It does NOT certify for many common EHR criteria such as medication lists (a)(6), allergy lists (a)(8), clinical decision support (a)(9), e-prescribing (a)(10)-(a)(11), or patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1).\n    23→\n    24→The CHPL product number format indicates this is certified by Drummond Group (ONC-ACB code \"04\").\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→OneConnect's intended users are **\"Post acute, rehab and other ambulatory clinicians\"** per its CHPL metadata. In practice, this means MedOne's own hospitalist physicians and advanced practice clinicians who provide medical services across approximately 150 post-acute facilities in Ohio.\n    29→\n    30→This is an **internally-developed, internally-used tool**. There is no evidence that OneConnect is sold or licensed to external customers. It supports MedOne's care model of embedding physician teams in skilled nursing facilities, assisted living communities, long-term acute care hospitals, and inpatient rehabilitation hospitals.\n    31→\n    32→Key deployment sites include OhioHealth Riverside Methodist Hospital, Genesis Hospital (Zanesville), Memorial Hospital (Marysville), Marietta Memorial Hospital, OhioHealth Rehabilitation Hospital, and Select Specialty Hospital (Columbus), plus approximately 150 post-acute facilities.\n    33→\n    34→### Modules & Functionality\n    35→\n    36→Based on the certification criteria and vendor disclosures, OneConnect provides:\n    37→\n    38→**Clinical Documentation:**\n    39→- The core function — streamlining clinical documentation for providers rounding in post-acute facilities\n    40→- Clinical notes (fast to generate, with cloud-based voice recognition, templates, and shorthand options per BOLT/MedOne Systems descriptions)\n    41→- Multiple users can work on the same patient record simultaneously\n    42→\n    43→**CPOE — Medications:**\n    44→- Certified for computerized provider order entry for medications, (a)(1)\n    45→- The BOLT platform (related product) claims physician order entry is \"up to five times faster than other leading EHRs\"\n    46→\n    47→**Patient Demographics & Device Tracking:**\n    48→- Demographics management (a)(5)\n    49→- Implantable device list tracking (a)(14)\n    50→\n    51→**Transitions of Care:**\n    52→- Certified for transitions of care (b)(1) — critical for a product used across post-acute settings\n    53→- Direct messaging support via EMR Direct Interoperability Engine 2017 (h)(1)\n    54→\n    55→**Clinical Quality Measures:**\n    56→- Certified for CQM recording and export (c)(1)\n    57→- 68 clinical quality measures (version 12) certified\n    58→\n    59→**FHIR APIs:**\n    60→- Standardized API for patient and population services (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n    61→\n    62→**Third-Party Integrations:**\n    63→- **RXNT** (Version 4.56) — used for e-prescribing services (separate fees apply)\n    64→- **EMR Direct Interoperability Engine 2017** (versions H.1 and B.1) — for Direct messaging / health information exchange\n    65→- **Medline Plus Connect NLM API** — for context-aware clinical knowledge retrieval\n    66→- **PCC (likely PointClickCare) integration** — monthly per-interface charges at facility level, confirming integration with post-acute facility EHRs\n    67→\n    68→**Billing/Coding (from related BOLT platform):**\n    69→- CPT code capture for procedures and notes\n    70→- Automatic export to accounting systems for billing\n    71→- (Note: it is unclear whether these BOLT-specific features are in OneConnect or are separate)\n    72→\n    73→**Clinical Safety (from related BOLT platform):**\n    74→- Automatic VTE risk assessment with treatment display\n    75→- Patient photos for safety identification\n    76→- Present-on-admission documentation reminders\n    77→\n    78→### Data & Content\n    79→\n    80→Based on the certified criteria, third-party dependencies, and vendor descriptions, OneConnect stores or manages:\n    81→\n    82→1. **Clinical notes/documentation** — the primary data type; provider documentation of patient encounters across post-acute settings\n    83→2. **Patient demographics** — certified (a)(5)\n    84→3. **Medication orders** — certified CPOE for medications (a)(1); e-prescribing via RXNT\n    85→4. **Implantable device information** — certified (a)(14)\n    86→5. **Transitions of care documents** — CCDAs or similar documents for care transitions (b)(1)\n    87→6. **Clinical quality measure data** — 68 CQMs certified (c)(1)\n    88→7. **Audit logs** — required for (d) criteria certification\n    89→8. **User/access data** — multi-factor authentication, access controls, etc.\n    90→\n    91→**What is NOT evident from the certification or vendor materials:**\n    92→- **No medication list management** — (a)(6) not certified\n    93→- **No allergy list management** — (a)(8) not certified\n    94→- **No clinical decision support** — (a)(9) not certified\n    95→- **No e-prescribing module** (uses third-party RXNT instead)\n    96→- **No patient portal** — (e)(1) not certified\n    97→- **No lab orders or results** — (a)(2), (a)(3) not certified\n    98→- **No vital signs management** — (a)(4) not certified\n    99→- **No problem list management** — (a)(7) not certified\n   100→- **No imaging** — (a)(12)-(a)(13) not certified\n   101→- **No public health reporting** — (f) criteria not certified (except indirectly through CQMs)\n   102→\n   103→The product's narrow certification scope is consistent with it being a focused documentation and workflow tool rather than a comprehensive EHR. The vendor's website does not describe billing data management, scheduling, messaging, or other common EHR functions as part of OneConnect specifically — though the related BOLT product does mention billing/coding features.\n   104→\n   105→**Important ambiguity:** The relationship between OneConnect (the certified product) and BOLT (the MedOne Systems product) is unclear. They may share code, data, or infrastructure. BOLT is described as an EHR overlay that enhances existing enterprise EHRs, while OneConnect is described as a documentation tool that integrates with facility EMRs. They could be the same product under different names, or genuinely separate tools serving different clinical settings (BOLT for acute hospital settings, OneConnect for post-acute settings).\n   106→\n   107→---\n   108→\n   109→## Related Product: BOLT (MedOne Systems)\n   110→\n   111→While not the certified product under review, BOLT is worth noting because it shares the same developer/founder and may share architecture or data with OneConnect.\n   112→\n   113→BOLT is described as \"a brilliantly simple software solution that enhances your existing enterprise EHR, augmenting its clinical functionality.\" It is designed for hospitalist workflows in acute care settings and includes:\n   114→- Fast clinical documentation with voice recognition\n   115→- Intelligent team assignment and rounding list management\n   116→- CPOE (claimed 5x faster than leading EHRs)\n   117→- VTE risk assessment and clinical safety features\n   118→- Billing/CPT code capture with accounting system export\n   119→- Multi-user concurrent charting\n   120→\n   121→MedOne Systems (~$3M revenue) develops and maintains BOLT. It is **not a traditional health IT vendor** — it is a clinical practice that built its own EHR software to support its operations. The organization has grown to be the largest hospital medicine practice in central Ohio, with over 100 physicians, 75+ advanced practice clinicians, and 40+ administrative staff. They partner with 4 acute hospital systems, 4 long-term acute care hospital partners, and approximately 150 skilled nursing, assisted living, and independent living facilities across Ohio.\n\nMedOne's founder, Dr. Joseph Mack, is both a physician and programmer. In 2012, he also founded a separate entity, **MedOne Systems, LLC** (based in Marietta, OH), which develops the **BOLT** clinical software platform — an EHR overlay/enhancement tool designed to augment existing enterprise EHRs. The certified product **OneConnect** appears to be a related but distinct tool from BOLT, specifically focused on post-acute care documentation and workflow. Both products share the same origin and development philosophy: clinician-built software designed for hospitalist and post-acute care workflows.\n\nMedOne Healthcare Partners is a very small vendor from a health IT certification perspective. OneConnect appears to be primarily used internally by MedOne's own clinicians to document care across the post-acute facilities they serve — it is not marketed as a commercial EHR product for external customers. The company has approximately $3M in annual revenue (MedOne Systems) and no presence on standard EHR review platforms (G2, KLAS, Capterra).\n\n## Product: OneConnect\n\nCHPL IDs: 11426\n\n### What It Is\n\nOneConnect is a **custom clinical documentation tool** designed for post-acute care settings. According to MedOne's own description, it is \"a custom documentation tool that enables providers to streamline workflows, communicate effectively and improve patient care and experience.\" Critically, it is **designed to integrate seamlessly with a post-acute facility's existing EMR** — meaning OneConnect is not a standalone full-featured EHR but rather a documentation overlay used by MedOne's physicians as they round across skilled nursing facilities, assisted living facilities, rehab hospitals, and long-term acute care hospitals.\n\nThe certified module (Version 0, certified 2023-12-27) covers a focused set of ONC criteria — 24 criteria in total, including CPOE for medications (a)(1), demographics (a)(5), implantable device list (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1), EHI export (b)(10), clinical quality measures (c)(1), standardized FHIR APIs (g)(7)/(g)(9)/(g)(10), and Direct messaging (h)(1). It does NOT certify for many common EHR criteria such as medication lists (a)(6), allergy lists (a)(8), clinical decision support (a)(9), e-prescribing (a)(10)-(a)(11), or patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1).\n\nThe CHPL product number format indicates this is certified by Drummond Group (ONC-ACB code \"04\").\n\n### Users & Market\n\nOneConnect's intended users are **\"Post acute, rehab and other ambulatory clinicians\"** per its CHPL metadata. In practice, this means MedOne's own hospitalist physicians and advanced practice clinicians who provide medical services across approximately 150 post-acute facilities in Ohio.\n\nThis is an **internally-developed, internally-used tool**. There is no evidence that OneConnect is sold or licensed to external customers. It supports MedOne's care model of embedding physician teams in skilled nursing facilities, assisted living communities, long-term acute care hospitals, and inpatient rehabilitation hospitals.\n\nKey deployment sites include OhioHealth Riverside Methodist Hospital, Genesis Hospital (Zanesville), Memorial Hospital (Marysville), Marietta Memorial Hospital, OhioHealth Rehabilitation Hospital, and Select Specialty Hospital (Columbus), plus approximately 150 post-acute facilities.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the certification criteria and vendor disclosures, OneConnect provides:\n\n**Clinical Documentation:**\n- The core function — streamlining clinical documentation for providers rounding in post-acute facilities\n- Clinical notes (fast to generate, with cloud-based voice recognition, templates, and shorthand options per BOLT/MedOne Systems descriptions)\n- Multiple users can work on the same patient record simultaneously\n\n**CPOE — Medications:**\n- Certified for computerized provider order entry for medications, (a)(1)\n- The BOLT platform (related product) claims physician order entry is \"up to five times faster than other leading EHRs\"\n\n**Patient Demographics & Device Tracking:**\n- Demographics management (a)(5)\n- Implantable device list tracking (a)(14)\n\n**Transitions of Care:**\n- Certified for transitions of care (b)(1) — critical for a product used across post-acute settings\n- Direct messaging support via EMR Direct Interoperability Engine 2017 (h)(1)\n\n**Clinical Quality Measures:**\n- Certified for CQM recording and export (c)(1)\n- 68 clinical quality measures (version 12) certified\n\n**FHIR APIs:**\n- Standardized API for patient and population services (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n\n**Third-Party Integrations:**\n- **RXNT** (Version 4.56) — used for e-prescribing services (separate fees apply)\n- **EMR Direct Interoperability Engine 2017** (versions H.1 and B.1) — for Direct messaging / health information exchange\n- **Medline Plus Connect NLM API** — for context-aware clinical knowledge retrieval\n- **PCC (likely PointClickCare) integration** — monthly per-interface charges at facility level, confirming integration with post-acute facility EHRs\n\n**Billing/Coding (from related BOLT platform):**\n- CPT code capture for procedures and notes\n- Automatic export to accounting systems for billing\n- (Note: it is unclear whether these BOLT-specific features are in OneConnect or are separate)\n\n**Clinical Safety (from related BOLT platform):**\n- Automatic VTE risk assessment with treatment display\n- Patient photos for safety identification\n- Present-on-admission documentation reminders\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the certified criteria, third-party dependencies, and vendor descriptions, OneConnect stores or manages:\n\n1. **Clinical notes/documentation** — the primary data type; provider documentation of patient encounters across post-acute settings\n2. **Patient demographics** — certified (a)(5)\n3. **Medication orders** — certified CPOE for medications (a)(1); e-prescribing via RXNT\n4. **Implantable device information** — certified (a)(14)\n5. **Transitions of care documents** — CCDAs or similar documents for care transitions (b)(1)\n6. **Clinical quality measure data** — 68 CQMs certified (c)(1)\n7. **Audit logs** — required for (d) criteria certification\n8. **User/access data** — multi-factor authentication, access controls, etc.\n\n**What is NOT evident from the certification or vendor materials:**\n- **No medication list management** — (a)(6) not certified\n- **No allergy list management** — (a)(8) not certified\n- **No clinical decision support** — (a)(9) not certified\n- **No e-prescribing module** (uses third-party RXNT instead)\n- **No patient portal** — (e)(1) not certified\n- **No lab orders or results** — (a)(2), (a)(3) not certified\n- **No vital signs management** — (a)(4) not certified\n- **No problem list management** — (a)(7) not certified\n- **No imaging** — (a)(12)-(a)(13) not certified\n- **No public health reporting** — (f) criteria not certified (except indirectly through CQMs)\n\nThe product's narrow certification scope is consistent with it being a focused documentation and workflow tool rather than a comprehensive EHR. The vendor's website does not describe billing data management, scheduling, messaging, or other common EHR functions as part of OneConnect specifically — though the related BOLT product does mention billing/coding features.\n\n**Important ambiguity:** The relationship between OneConnect (the certified product) and BOLT (the MedOne Systems product) is unclear. They may share code, data, or infrastructure. BOLT is described as an EHR overlay that enhances existing enterprise EHRs, while OneConnect is described as a documentation tool that integrates with facility EMRs. They could be the same product under different names, or genuinely separate tools serving different clinical settings (BOLT for acute hospital settings, OneConnect for post-acute settings).\n\n---\n\n## Related Product: BOLT (MedOne Systems)\n\nWhile not the certified product under review, BOLT is worth noting because it shares the same developer/founder and may share architecture or data with OneConnect.\n\nBOLT is described as \"a brilliantly simple software solution that enhances your existing enterprise EHR, augmenting its clinical functionality.\" It is designed for hospitalist workflows in acute care settings and includes:\n- Fast clinical documentation with voice recognition\n- Intelligent team assignment and rounding list management\n- CPOE (claimed 5x faster than leading EHRs)\n- VTE risk assessment and clinical safety features\n- Billing/CPT code capture with accounting system export\n- Multi-user concurrent charting\n\nMedOne Systems (~$3M revenue) develops and maintains BOLT. It is unclear whether BOLT and OneConnect share a common codebase or database.\n","numLines":122,"startLine":1,"totalLines":122}}}

Its full text:\n\n> **Product Name and Version: OneConnect Version 0**\n>\n> The patient EHI export contains data from the patient's chart. Multiple file formats are used to store this information.\n>\n> ZIP is an archive file format.\n>\n> PDF or Portable Document Format is a file format that is used to present text or image based documents.\n>\n> C-CDA or Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture is a file format used for health information exchange.\n>\n> The export file itself is a zip file. It contains zip files of C-CDAs for now and other files attached to the patient's chart will be added later (e.g. PDF and images)\n>\n> Information for each patient encounter is available C-CDA format in zip archive.\n\nThat is the entire document. It describes an export that produces a ZIP file containing:\n- C-CDA documents (one per encounter)\n- Promises that \"other files attached to the patient's chart will be added later (e.g. PDF and images)\"\n\nThere is **no data dictionary**, **no schema**, **no field-level documentation**, **no sample export**, and **no user instructions** for performing the export.\n\n### FHIR API Specifications PDF (58 pages)\n\nThis is the (g)(10) Standardized API documentation — a Smart on FHIR implementation guide covering OAuth2 authentication, patient-level FHIR resource access (AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Conditions, ImplantableDevice, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Labs, Goals, Immunizations, Medications, SmokingStatus, Procedures, Provenance, VitalSigns), patient search, encounter search, and CCDA retrieval. It ends with terms of use. This is **not** the (b)(10) EHI export documentation — it documents the standard FHIR API for individual patient access and population-level services.\n\n### FHIR Valid URLs JSON\n\nA FHIR Bundle containing a single Endpoint resource pointing to `https://qafhir.medonehp.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/` and an Organization resource. This is infrastructure metadata for the FHIR API, not related to the EHI export mechanism.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe EHI export documentation says the export \"contains data from the patient's chart\" and delivers it as C-CDA documents per encounter. Based on the product research, OneConnect stores:\n\n**Likely covered by C-CDA (standard C-CDA sections):**\n- Patient demographics\n- Medications/orders (CPOE certified)\n- Implantable device information\n- Clinical notes (the product's core function)\n- Transitions of care data\n\n**Almost certainly missing or inadequately covered:**\n- **Billing/CPT codes** — The related BOLT platform captures CPT codes and exports to accounting systems. C-CDA does not have a standard billing section. No mention of billing data in the export documentation.\n- **Custom clinical documentation** — OneConnect's primary value is streamlined clinical documentation with templates, voice recognition, and shorthand. C-CDA imposes a rigid structure that likely cannot faithfully represent the vendor's custom documentation workflows and formats.\n- **Quality measure data** — 68 CQMs are certified; no mention of how this data is exported.\n- **Provider order entry details** — Beyond what C-CDA captures in its standard medication sections.\n\n**Ambiguous / cannot determine:**\n- The scope claim is broad (\"data from the patient's chart\") but the format (C-CDA per encounter) inherently limits what can be represented. The document explicitly states additional content types \"will be added later,\" implying the current export is incomplete.\n- There is no mention of whether the export includes data from integrated systems (e.g., RXNT e-prescribing data, data received via Direct messaging, data from PCC/PointClickCare integration).\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses **C-CDA** documents packaged in a ZIP archive, one C-CDA per encounter. C-CDA is a recognized standard, but it is a **clinical summary standard**, not an EHI bulk export format. C-CDA is designed for transitions of care and patient summaries — it captures a defined set of clinical sections but is not designed to represent the full breadth of electronic health information a system stores.\n\nThis is a classic case of a vendor using their (g)(10)/(b)(1) clinical document infrastructure to satisfy (b)(10). The C-CDA will cover standard clinical data elements, but it is structurally incapable of representing:\n- Billing records and charge data\n- Custom form/template data that doesn't map to C-CDA sections\n- System-specific workflow data used in patient care decisions\n- Data relationships and metadata beyond what C-CDA supports\n\nA third party receiving this export would get a set of clinical encounter summaries but would not be able to reconstruct the full patient record as it exists in OneConnect.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Extremely poor.** The entire EHI export documentation is 6 sentences on a single page. It:\n- Defines what ZIP, PDF, and C-CDA are (which is unhelpful)\n- States the export is a ZIP of C-CDAs\n- Acknowledges the export is incomplete (\"will be added later\")\n- Provides no instructions for performing the export\n- Provides no data dictionary or field mapping\n- Provides no sample data\n- Provides no information about what C-CDA sections are populated\n- Provides no information about how to request an export\n\nA developer could not implement an import of this data based on this documentation alone. They would need to know which C-CDA template is used, what sections are populated, what coded values appear, and how encounters are organized in the ZIP structure — none of which is documented.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThere is essentially no structured documentation. The document does not contain:\n- Table or field definitions\n- Data types or value sets\n- Cardinality or constraints\n- Entity relationships\n- Schema files or machine-readable artifacts\n- Versioning or change history\n- Sample files\n\nThis appears to be the absolute minimum compliance artifact — a document that acknowledges the existence of an EHI export capability without meaningfully documenting it.\n\n### Context\n\nThis is consistent with what the product research revealed: MedOne Healthcare Partners is a physician practice that built its own EHR, not a health IT vendor. OneConnect is used internally by ~100 physicians and 75+ APCs. The documentation quality reflects an organization that built software for internal use and obtained certification as a regulatory requirement, not as a commercial product feature. The phrase \"will be added later\" in a document dated November 2023 (over two years ago) suggests the export capability was still under development at certification time and the documentation was never updated.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://m8osrnd4strxadl3.public.blob.vercel-storage.com/EHI%20Export.pdf (found via certifications page)\n- Registered URL status: dead (HTTP 500 — site rebuilt on new platform)\n- Required browser: no (curl works for all downloads)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (from certifications page footer)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The registered CHPL URL (https://www.medonehp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/Export.pdf) returns HTTP 500. The site was rebuilt from WordPress to Next.js/Vercel/Payload CMS, breaking the old wp-content paths.\n- The certifications page at https://www.medonehp.com/certifications/ is the current location of the documentation links, hosted on Vercel blob storage.\n- The FHIR API Documentation link in the Interoperability section points to a Wayback Machine URL (https://web.archive.org/web/20250805232450/https://www.medonehp.com/fhir/SmartOnFHIR-API.pdf), suggesting the original on-site link also broke during the platform migration and they patched it with an archive.org link rather than updating the file location.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":512,"cache_read_input_tokens":63307,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":512},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9cf0a6aa-565c-4339-9e92-d89096389aea","uuid":"41e11773-f031-4e8b-a933-dd49a337eb60"}
